STAGE-25 → PHASE-06 → STEP-08
Workspace Persistence Platform

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-08 establishes the Workspace Persistence Platform, the institutional layer responsible for preserving workspace runtime state across operator sessions.

While the previous Workspace Platform layers define workspace structure, composition, interaction, and layout, STEP-08 introduces the governance required to persist those runtime configurations without coupling the Workspace Platform to any specific storage technology.

This completes the Workspace Infrastructure Layer and prepares the platform for the Workspace Runtime Orchestrator in the final step of Phase-06.

2. Architectural Position
Architecture
        │
        ▼
Foundation Platform
        │
        ▼
Provider Platform
        │
        ▼
Capability Platform
        │
        ▼
Workspace Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Panel Platform
        ├── Layout Platform
        ├── Docking Platform
        ├── Persistence Platform
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Persistence Platform sits directly above the Docking Platform and immediately below the Workspace Runtime Orchestrator.

3. Purpose

The Workspace Persistence Platform answers the institutional question:

"How does COREI preserve an operator's workspace across sessions while remaining independent of storage technology?"

Persistence is an institutional responsibility.

It is not:

IndexedDB
LocalStorage
PostgreSQL
SQLite
Filesystem
Cloud synchronization

Those are implementation providers that will be integrated through the Provider Platform.

4. Workspace Persistence Components

The Workspace Persistence Platform introduces five institutional governance components.

Workspace Persistence Contract

Defines the canonical persistence contract for every workspace snapshot.

Responsibilities include:

workspace identity
associated layout
persistence version

The contract standardizes every persisted workspace regardless of storage implementation.

Workspace Persistence Model

Represents the runtime persistence definition.

The model extends the Workspace Persistence Contract and becomes the canonical runtime representation stored within the persistence registry.

Workspace Persistence Registry

Provides the authoritative repository for workspace persistence definitions.

Responsibilities include:

persistence registration
persistence discovery
persistence enumeration
centralized governance

The registry becomes the single source of truth for persistence metadata.

Workspace Persistence Manager

Provides operational interaction with the persistence registry.

Responsibilities include:

registering persistence definitions
retrieving persistence definitions
managing persistence collections

Operational logic remains isolated from storage concerns.

Workspace Persistence Runtime

Defines the runtime state of the Persistence Platform.

Current responsibility:

initialization status

Future phases will extend runtime behavior with save, restore, synchronization, migration, and recovery services.

5. Relationship with the Docking Platform

The Persistence Platform consumes runtime interaction state produced by the Docking Platform.

Runtime relationship:

Workspace
        │
        ▼
Composition
        │
        ▼
Panels
        │
        ▼
Layout
        │
        ▼
Docking
        │
        ▼
Persistence

The Persistence Platform never controls docking.

It only preserves docking state.

6. Relationship with the Provider Platform

One of the primary architectural objectives of STEP-08 is maintaining storage independence.

Future implementation flow:

Workspace Persistence
        │
        ▼
Persistence Provider
        │
        ▼
Storage Technology

Examples:

Workspace Persistence
        │
        ├── IndexedDB Provider
        ├── PostgreSQL Provider
        ├── Browser Storage Provider
        ├── Local File Provider
        └── Cloud Sync Provider

Replacing a storage backend requires only a new provider implementation.

No Workspace Platform code changes.

        │

        │
        ▼

        │

        │

Operator sees identical workspace

This enables deterministic restoration of operator environments.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-06 → STEP-09
Workspace Runtime Orchestrator

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-09 establishes the Workspace Runtime Orchestrator, the institutional execution layer responsible for coordinating every Workspace Platform subsystem into a single operational runtime.

While the previous steps established the individual building blocks of the Workspace Platform, STEP-09 integrates those subsystems into a deterministic execution pipeline capable of constructing, restoring, activating, suspending, resuming, deactivating, and shutting down complete COREI workspaces.

This marks the completion of the Workspace Platform and provides the first fully orchestrated operational platform within the COREI Operating System.

2. Architectural Position
Architecture
        │
        ▼
Foundation Platform
        │
        ▼
Provider Platform
        │
        ▼
Capability Platform
        │
        ▼
Workspace Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Panel Platform
        ├── Layout Platform
        ├── Docking Platform
        ├── Persistence Platform
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator is the highest-level component within the Workspace Platform and serves as the single execution entry point for workspace operations.

3. Purpose

The Workspace Runtime Orchestrator answers the institutional question:

"How does COREI execute an entire workspace as a coordinated operational system?"

The Runtime Orchestrator is not responsible for:

rendering UI
executing business logic
managing providers
storing persistence
implementing layouts

Instead, it coordinates all Workspace Platform subsystems according to a deterministic lifecycle.

4. Workspace Runtime Components

The Runtime Platform introduces two institutional governance components.

Workspace Runtime

Defines the canonical runtime lifecycle for every workspace.

The runtime contract establishes the operational lifecycle methods:

initialize()
restore()
activate()
suspend()
resume()
deactivate()
shutdown()

These methods define the complete lifecycle of a workspace without exposing implementation details.

Workspace Runtime Orchestrator

Provides centralized execution coordination for the Workspace Platform.

Responsibilities include:

workspace initialization
workspace restoration
runtime activation
runtime suspension
runtime resumption
runtime deactivation
workspace shutdown

The orchestrator becomes the single operational controller for workspace execution.

5. Relationship with the Workspace Platform

The Runtime Orchestrator coordinates every subsystem established throughout Phase-06.

Runtime relationship:

Workspace Runtime Orchestrator
        │
        ├── Workspace Lifecycle
        ├── Workspace State
        ├── Workspace Composition
        ├── Panel Platform
        ├── Layout Platform
        ├── Docking Platform
        └── Persistence Platform

Each subsystem retains its own responsibility while the orchestrator coordinates their execution.

The Runtime Orchestrator activates workspaces rather than capabilities directly.

Execution hierarchy:

Workspace Runtime
        │
        ▼
Workspace
        │
        ▼

        │
        ▼

        │
        ▼
Providers

        │

        │
        ▼
Provider Platform
        │
        ▼
Technology Providers

This ensures that provider implementations remain fully encapsulated behind institutional abstractions.

Next Step

We should now begin with:

STAGE-25 → PHASE-07 → STEP-01 — Experience Foundation Implementation

This will establish the institutional foundation for every COREI experience, following the same disciplined progression that successfully built the Provider, Capability, and Workspace platforms. From there, each subsequent step will layer lifecycle, state, composition, bindings, and orchestration into a complete Experience Platform.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-07 → STEP-01
Experience Foundation

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-01 establishes the Experience Foundation, the institutional platform responsible for defining the canonical representation of an operational experience within the COREI Operating System.

An Experience represents the highest operational abstraction below the COREI Operating System. It assembles workspaces, capabilities, providers, runtime services, and operator context into a unified environment while remaining completely independent of implementation technologies.

This foundation enables every future operational environment—Trading, Infrastructure, Risk, Research, Executive, Compliance, and others—to inherit a common governance model.

2. Architectural Position
Architecture
        │
        ▼
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ▼
Workspace Platform
        │
        ▼
Capability Platform
        │
        ▼
Provider Platform
        │
        ▼
Foundation Platform
        │
        ▼
Technology Providers

The Experience Platform becomes the consumer of all previously completed Stage-25 platforms.

3. Purpose

The Experience Foundation answers the institutional question:

"What is an operational experience within COREI?"

An Experience is not:

a React application
a page
a workspace
a UI theme
a provider

Instead, it is an institutional operational environment that coordinates:

operator context
workspace selection
capability availability
provider utilization
runtime execution
4. Experience Platform Components

The Experience Foundation introduces five institutional governance components.

Experience Contract

Defines the canonical contract for every operational experience.

Responsibilities include:

experience identity
experience name
description
operational enablement

The contract establishes the common interface shared by all experiences.

Experience Model

Represents the runtime definition of an experience.

The model extends the Experience Contract and becomes the canonical runtime representation stored within the registry.

Experience Registry

Provides the authoritative repository for every registered experience.

Responsibilities include:

experience registration
experience discovery
experience enumeration
centralized governance

The registry becomes the single source of truth for operational experiences.

Experience Manager

Provides operational interaction with the Experience Registry.

Responsibilities include:

registering experiences
retrieving experiences
managing experience collections

Operational logic remains separated from registry storage.

Experience Runtime

Defines the runtime state of the Experience Platform.

Current responsibility:

initialization status

Future steps will extend this runtime with lifecycle orchestration and operational coordination.

5. Relationship with the Workspace Platform

The Experience Platform consumes complete workspaces.

Runtime relationship:

Experience
        │
        ▼
Workspace Platform
        │
        ▼
Workspace Runtime

The Experience Platform never replaces workspace responsibilities.

It assembles and governs them.

6. Relationship with the Capability Platform

Each Experience exposes a curated set of capabilities.

Execution hierarchy:

Experience
        │
        ▼
Workspace
        │
        ▼
Capabilities
        │
        ▼
Providers

The Experience Platform does not implement capabilities directly.

It determines which capabilities are available within a given operational environment.

7. Relationship with the Provider Platform

Provider technologies remain fully encapsulated.

Execution flow:

Experience
        │
        ▼
Capability Platform
        │
        ▼
Provider Platform
        │
        ▼
Technology Providers

The Experience Platform never communicates directly with implementation technologies.

8. Existing Experience Implementations

The repository already contains experience-specific implementation folders that consume the institutional platform.

Current implementations include:

src/experiences/

├── trading/
│   ├── components/
│   ├── hooks/
│   ├── integration/
│   └── providers/
│
└── infrastructure/
    ├── components/
    ├── hooks/
    ├── integration/
    └── providers/

These are experience implementations, whereas STEP-01 establishes the institutional Experience Platform that governs them.

This separation is intentional:

Experience Platform
        │
        ├── Trading Experience
        │
        ├── Infrastructure Experience
        │
        ├── Risk Experience
        │
        ├── Research Experience
        │
        ├── Executive Experience
        │
        └── Compliance Experience

The platform provides governance; individual experiences provide implementation.

9. Repository Organization

The institutional Experience Platform has been organized under:

src/experiences/

Current foundational structure:

contracts/
models/
registry/
manager/
runtime/
index.ts

Experience implementations currently coexist under:

trading/
infrastructure/

This architecture preserves a clean separation between framework and implementation.

10. Architectural Separation

The Experience Foundation owns:

experience contracts
experience models
experience registry
experience manager
experience runtime

It does not own:

workspace implementation
capability implementation
provider implementation
UI rendering
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to the Workspace, Capability, and Provider Platforms.

11. Dependency Rules

The Experience Platform follows the approved COREI dependency hierarchy.

Allowed:

        │

Workspace Platform
        │

        │

Provider Platform

Forbidden:

        │
        ▼
Vendor Libraries

Forbidden:

Experience Platform
        │
        ▼
Browser APIs

All implementation technologies remain isolated behind institutional abstractions.

12. Architectural Benefits

The Experience Foundation provides:

standardized operational environment abstraction
centralized experience governance
reusable experience definitions
provider-independent execution
scalable multi-experience architecture
deterministic operational boundaries

New experiences can be introduced without altering lower architectural layers.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-07 → STEP-02
Experience Lifecycle

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-02 establishes the Experience Lifecycle, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the operational lifecycle of every COREI Experience.

While STEP-01 introduced the Experience Foundation, STEP-02 defines how an experience progresses through its operational states from initialization to shutdown.

The Experience Lifecycle is independent of UI frameworks, browser events, React component lifecycles, or provider implementations. It governs only the institutional operational lifecycle of an Experience.

2. Architectural Position
Architecture
        │
        ▼
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Registry
        ├── Manager
        ├── Workspace Binding
        ├── Capability Binding
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Lifecycle subsystem extends the Experience Foundation by introducing deterministic operational transitions.

3. Purpose

The Experience Lifecycle answers the institutional question:

"How does an operational experience transition through its complete execution lifecycle?"

The lifecycle is not:

React component lifecycle
Browser lifecycle
Provider lifecycle
Workspace lifecycle
Capability lifecycle

Instead, it defines the institutional lifecycle for the Experience itself.

4. Experience Lifecycle Components

The Experience Lifecycle introduces two institutional governance components.

Experience Lifecycle Contract

Defines the canonical lifecycle interface for every Experience.

Supported lifecycle operations:

initialize()
activate()
suspend()
resume()
deactivate()
shutdown()

These operations define the complete operational lifecycle while remaining implementation-independent.

Default Experience Lifecycle

Provides the default institutional implementation of the Experience Lifecycle.

Current implementation supplies deterministic lifecycle methods that will later coordinate Workspace, Capability, and Provider execution.

The default implementation intentionally contains no business logic.

5. Lifecycle Semantics

Each lifecycle operation has a distinct institutional responsibility.

initialize()

Responsible for preparing an Experience for execution.

Typical future responsibilities include:

configuration loading
dependency validation
workspace preparation
runtime initialization
activate()

Transitions the Experience into operational execution.

Typical future responsibilities include:

workspace activation
runtime coordination
capability enablement
operator readiness
suspend()

Temporarily pauses execution while preserving runtime state.

Typical future responsibilities include:

background processing
session preservation
resource optimization
resume()

Restores a suspended Experience to operational execution.

Typical future responsibilities include:

workspace restoration
runtime resynchronization
operator continuity
deactivate()

Terminates operational execution while preserving persistence.

Typical future responsibilities include:

workspace cleanup
capability shutdown
runtime deactivation
shutdown()

Performs the complete institutional shutdown of the Experience.

Typical future responsibilities include:

persistence finalization
resource release
runtime termination
6. Relationship with the Experience Foundation

The Experience Lifecycle extends the Experience Foundation.

Runtime relationship:

Experience Foundation
        │
        ▼
Experience Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Operational Execution

The Foundation defines what an Experience is.

The Lifecycle defines how it behaves over time.

7. Relationship with the Workspace Platform

The Experience Lifecycle coordinates workspaces but does not replace Workspace Lifecycle responsibilities.

Execution hierarchy:

Experience Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Workspace Runtime
        │
        ▼
Workspace Lifecycle

This preserves the architectural separation between Experience governance and Workspace execution.

8. Relationship with the Capability Platform

Capabilities remain subordinate to the Experience Lifecycle.

Execution flow:

Experience Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Workspace Runtime
        │
        ▼
Capability Runtime

The Experience Lifecycle governs overall execution without implementing capability behavior.

index.ts

Each file follows the COREI institutional principle of single responsibility.

10. Architectural Separation

The Experience Lifecycle owns:

lifecycle contract
lifecycle transitions
operational execution stages
default lifecycle implementation

It does not own:

workspace implementation
capability implementation
provider initialization
UI rendering
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to lower architectural platforms.

The Experience Lifecycle follows the approved COREI dependency hierarchy.

        │

React Lifecycle

Forbidden:

Experience Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Browser Events

Forbidden:

Experience Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Vendor Providers

This preserves provider independence and institutional layering.

12. Architectural Benefits

The Experience Lifecycle provides:

standardized operational lifecycle
deterministic execution stages
provider-independent lifecycle management
reusable lifecycle abstraction
scalable operational governance
consistent execution model across all experiences

Every Experience follows the same lifecycle regardless of implementation details.

The Experience Lifecycle is now institutionally established and validated. It defines the deterministic operational execution model for every COREI Experience while preserving strict architectural separation from Workspace, Capability, and Provider platforms. With this subsystem complete, the Experience Platform is prepared for STEP-03, where the Experience State subsystem will introduce institutional state management, including operational status, health, readiness, and availability for every Experience.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-07 → STEP-03
Experience State

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-03 establishes the Experience State Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for representing the authoritative operational state of every COREI Experience.

While STEP-02 defined how an Experience progresses through its lifecycle, STEP-03 defines what its current operational condition is at any given moment.

The Experience State is the canonical operational state exposed by the Experience Platform. It is independent of UI state management libraries, browser memory, and implementation-specific runtime state.

2. Architectural Position
Architecture
        │
        ▼
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Registry
        ├── Manager
        ├── Workspace Binding
        ├── Capability Binding
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The State subsystem complements the Lifecycle subsystem by providing the authoritative operational status of an Experience.

3. Purpose

The Experience State answers the institutional question:

"What is the current operational condition of an Experience?"

The Experience State is not:

React state
Zustand store
Redux state
Browser memory
Component state
Provider runtime state

Instead, it is the institutional operational state owned by the Experience Platform.

4. Experience State Components

The Experience State Platform introduces five institutional governance components.

Experience Availability

Represents whether an Experience is operationally available.

Supported values:

AVAILABLE
UNAVAILABLE

Availability indicates whether an Experience can be selected and executed by the COREI Operating System.

Experience Health

Represents the operational health of an Experience.

Supported values:

HEALTHY
DEGRADED
FAILED

Health communicates the overall operational condition without exposing implementation details.

Experience Readiness

Represents whether an Experience is prepared for execution.

Supported values:

READY
NOT_READY

Readiness is evaluated before lifecycle activation.

Experience Status

Represents the current lifecycle stage of an Experience.

Supported values:

CREATED
INITIALIZED
ACTIVE
SUSPENDED
INACTIVE
SHUTDOWN

Status reflects the current operational phase defined by the Experience Lifecycle.

Experience State

Aggregates all operational state information into a single institutional model.

The state contains:

status
health
readiness
availability

This becomes the authoritative state representation consumed by higher-level orchestration.

5. Relationship with the Experience Lifecycle

The Lifecycle defines transitions.

The State defines the current condition.

Relationship:

Experience Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Experience State

Lifecycle methods update the institutional state while preserving separation of responsibilities.

6. Relationship with the Workspace Platform

Experience State does not replace Workspace State.

Execution hierarchy:

Experience State
        │
        ▼
Workspace State

The Experience aggregates workspace health without owning workspace implementation.

7. Relationship with the Capability Platform

Capabilities maintain independent operational state.

Relationship:

Experience State
        │
        ▼
Capability State

The Experience Platform consumes capability information but does not redefine capability state management.

Each file follows the COREI institutional principle of single responsibility.

The Experience State owns:

operational status
health
readiness
availability
institutional state aggregation

It does not own:

lifecycle execution
workspace implementation
capability implementation
provider state
UI rendering
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to their respective platforms.

Experience Foundation

Forbidden:

Forbidden:

Experience State
        │

Forbidden:

Experience State

11. Architectural Benefits

The Experience State provides:

authoritative operational state
standardized health reporting

reusable institutional state model

Every Experience exposes the same operational state model regardless of implementation.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-07 → STEP-04
Experience Composition

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-04 establishes the Experience Composition Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining how an operational Experience is assembled.

While previous steps defined:

what an Experience is,
how it executes,
and what operational state it exposes,

STEP-04 defines what components collectively form an Experience.

The Experience Composition Platform provides a deterministic composition model without instantiating or executing any implementation.

2. Architectural Position
Architecture
        │
        ▼
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Registry
        ├── Manager
        ├── Workspace Binding
        ├── Capability Binding
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Composition subsystem bridges architectural definitions with future runtime orchestration.

3. Purpose

The Experience Composition Platform answers the institutional question:

"What institutional components collectively make up an Experience?"

Composition is not:

runtime execution
dependency injection
provider initialization
workspace activation
capability loading

Instead, it defines the static architectural blueprint of an Experience.

4. Experience Composition Components

The Experience Composition Platform introduces four institutional governance components.

Experience Workspace Binding

Defines which workspaces belong to an Experience.

Each binding identifies:

Workspace identifier
Required/optional participation

The binding is declarative and does not construct the workspace.

Experience Capability Binding

Defines which capabilities are available within an Experience.

Each binding identifies:

Capability identifier
Required/optional participation

Capabilities remain independently governed by the Capability Platform.

Experience Provider Binding

Defines which provider abstractions are required by an Experience.

Each binding identifies:

Provider identifier
Required/optional participation

Providers remain independently governed by the Provider Platform.

Experience Composition

Aggregates every institutional binding into a single architectural model.

The composition contains:

Workspace bindings
Capability bindings
Provider bindings

This becomes the authoritative composition blueprint for every Experience.

5. Relationship with the Experience Foundation

The Foundation defines the Experience.

The Composition defines its institutional structure.

Relationship:

Experience Foundation
        │
        ▼
Experience Composition

The Foundation establishes identity.

The Composition establishes structure.

6. Relationship with the Workspace Platform

The Experience Composition Platform consumes Workspace definitions without owning them.

Relationship:

Experience Composition
        │
        ▼
Workspace Platform

The Workspace Platform remains responsible for workspace implementation and execution.

7. Relationship with the Capability Platform

Capabilities are referenced through bindings.

Relationship:

Experience Composition
        │
        ▼
Capability Platform

The Experience Platform declares capability participation without redefining capability behavior.

8. Relationship with the Provider Platform

Providers remain abstract implementation services.

Relationship:

Experience Composition
        │
        ▼
Provider Platform

The Experience Platform references providers while preserving implementation independence.

17. Practical End-to-End Composition Example

A future Trading Experience may be described as:

Trading Experience
        │
        ├── Workspaces
        │      ├── Trading Workspace
        │      ├── Market Workspace
        │      └── Research Workspace
        │
        ├── Capabilities
        │      ├── Chart Capability
        │      ├── Workflow Capability
        │      ├── Terminal Capability
        │      └── Grid Capability
        │
        └── Providers
               ├── ECharts Provider
               ├── Xterm Provider
               ├── Monaco Provider
               ├── AG Grid Provider
               └── React Flow Provider

An Infrastructure Experience can define a different composition while reusing the same institutional contracts:

Infrastructure Experience
        │
        ├── Workspaces
        │      ├── Infrastructure Workspace
        │      ├── Operations Workspace
        │      └── Monitoring Workspace
        │
        ├── Capabilities
        │      ├── Terminal Capability
        │      ├── Workflow Capability
        │      └── Visualization Capability
        │
        └── Providers
               ├── Xterm Provider
               ├── ECharts Provider
               └── React Flow Provider

This demonstrates that composition is declarative and independent of runtime execution.

18. Long-Term Architectural Alignment

The Experience Composition Platform aligns with COREI's institutional architecture by ensuring:

Every Experience has a deterministic architectural blueprint.
Workspaces, Capabilities, and Providers remain independently governed while being assembled through composition.
New Experiences can be introduced by declaring new compositions instead of altering platform internals.
Future runtime orchestration, dependency resolution, lazy loading, and experience packaging can consume the composition model without modifying its contracts.
The Experience Platform continues to preserve strict separation between governance, composition, execution, and implementation.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-07 → STEP-05
Experience Runtime

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-05 establishes the Experience Runtime Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the operational runtime contract of every COREI Experience.

While the previous steps established the identity, lifecycle, state, and composition of an Experience, STEP-05 defines the canonical runtime interface through which every Experience will eventually execute.

The Experience Runtime is intentionally implementation-independent. It does not execute business logic or coordinate lower-level platforms. Instead, it establishes the standardized operational contract that will later be orchestrated by the Experience Runtime Orchestrator.

2. Architectural Position
Architecture
        │
        ▼
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Registry
        ├── Manager
        ├── Workspace Binding
        ├── Capability Binding
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime subsystem bridges the static architectural model with future operational execution.

3. Purpose

The Experience Runtime answers the institutional question:

"What runtime operations does every Experience expose?"

The Runtime is not:

React runtime
Browser runtime
JavaScript execution context
Workspace Runtime
Capability Runtime
Provider Runtime

Instead, it is the institutional runtime abstraction for the Experience Platform.

4. Experience Runtime Components

The Runtime subsystem introduces two institutional governance components.

Experience Runtime Contract

Defines the canonical operational interface for every Experience.

Supported runtime operations:

initialize()
load()
activate()
suspend()
resume()
unload()
shutdown()

These methods represent the complete operational lifecycle expected of every Experience while remaining independent of implementation technologies.

Default Experience Runtime

Provides the default institutional implementation of the Experience Runtime contract.

The default implementation supplies deterministic runtime methods without embedding business logic, provider interactions, or workspace orchestration.

Future runtime coordination will be introduced by the Experience Runtime Orchestrator.

5. Runtime Semantics

Each runtime operation has a distinct institutional responsibility.

initialize()

Prepares the Experience runtime for execution.

Future responsibilities may include:

runtime initialization
dependency preparation
configuration loading
operational context creation
load()

Loads the institutional resources required by the Experience.

Future responsibilities may include:

workspace loading
capability preparation
runtime resource acquisition
activate()

Transitions the Experience into active operational execution.

Future responsibilities may include:

workspace activation
capability enablement
operator session activation
suspend()

Temporarily pauses the operational session while preserving runtime state.

Future responsibilities may include:

resource throttling
session preservation
operational pause
resume()

Restores a suspended Experience to active execution.

Future responsibilities may include:

session restoration
workspace resynchronization
runtime continuation
unload()

Releases operational resources while preserving institutional persistence where appropriate.

Future responsibilities may include:

workspace unloading
capability cleanup
runtime resource release
shutdown()

Terminates the Experience runtime completely.

Future responsibilities may include:

final persistence
resource disposal
runtime termination
6. Relationship with the Experience Lifecycle

The Experience Lifecycle defines operational transitions.

The Experience Runtime defines runtime execution operations.

Relationship:

Experience Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Experience Runtime

The Runtime executes within the lifecycle boundaries while remaining a separate institutional subsystem.

7. Relationship with the Workspace Platform

The Experience Runtime consumes Workspace Runtime services without replacing them.

Execution hierarchy:

Experience Runtime
        │
        ▼
Workspace Runtime

Each Workspace retains independent execution responsibility.

8. Relationship with the Capability Platform

Capabilities execute within the Experience Runtime.

Relationship:

Experience Runtime
        │
        ▼
Capability Runtime

The Experience Runtime governs overall execution without implementing capability behavior.

9. Relationship with the Provider Platform

Provider technologies remain fully encapsulated.

Execution hierarchy:

Experience Runtime
        │
        ▼
Capability Runtime
        │
        ▼
Provider Runtime

The Experience Runtime never communicates directly with implementation technologies.

src/experiences/runtime/

Current repository structure:

experience-runtime.ts

index.ts

The Runtime contract remains compact, provider-independent, and focused solely on institutional execution responsibilities.

11. Architectural Separation

The Experience Runtime owns:

runtime contract
operational runtime methods
execution interface
runtime abstraction

It does not own:

runtime orchestration
workspace management
capability management
provider implementation
UI rendering
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to their respective platforms and future orchestration layers.

        │

        │

Forbidden:

Experience Runtime
        │

Browser APIs

Forbidden:

Experience Runtime
        │
        ▼
Technology Providers

This preserves institutional abstraction and provider independence.

standardized runtime interface
deterministic operational contract
provider-independent execution model
reusable runtime abstraction
scalable execution architecture
clear separation between runtime and orchestration

Future runtime enhancements can be introduced without changing the contract.

This standardized runtime model ensures that every Experience executes consistently while allowing each implementation to provide domain-specific behavior behind a common institutional contract.

1. Objective

        │      └── Enabled  : Yes

               ├── Required : No
               └── Enabled  : Yes

Likewise, an Infrastructure Experience can declare a different provider set while reusing the same institutional integration contracts.

The Experience Platform defines provider participation only. Provider initialization, runtime behavior, lifecycle management, and implementation remain exclusively owned by the Provider Platform.

Cross-platform relationships remain deterministic and implementation-independent.
The Experience Platform continues to preserve strict separation between composition, integration, execution, and orchestration.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-07 → STEP-09
Experience Runtime Graph

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-09 establishes the Experience Runtime Graph Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the deterministic execution topology of every COREI Experience.

While previous steps defined the Experience itself, its lifecycle, state, composition, runtime interface, and cross-platform integrations, STEP-09 introduces the authoritative dependency graph that organizes these elements into an executable structure.

The Runtime Graph is intentionally declarative. It models execution relationships without performing execution.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Workspace Integration
        ├── Capability Integration
        ├── Provider Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph becomes the architectural bridge between integration definitions and runtime orchestration.

3. Purpose

The Experience Runtime Graph answers the institutional question:

"What is the deterministic execution topology of an Experience?"

It is not:

Runtime Orchestrator
Dependency Injection Container
Workflow Engine
Event Engine
Scheduler
Runtime Executor

Those responsibilities remain outside the Runtime Graph.

The Runtime Graph exists solely to model execution topology.

4. Runtime Graph Components

The Runtime Graph Platform introduces four institutional governance components.

Runtime Graph Node

Represents a single executable resource within an Experience.

Each node defines:

unique identifier
resource type
dependency references

Nodes are generic and may represent Workspaces, Capabilities, Providers, or future Experience resources.

Runtime Graph Edge

Defines the directional relationship between two Runtime Graph Nodes.

Each edge specifies:

source node
target node

Edges establish execution dependencies while remaining independent of execution logic.

Runtime Graph

Aggregates all Runtime Graph Nodes and Runtime Graph Edges into a single deterministic execution model.

The graph contains:

node collection
edge collection

This becomes the authoritative execution topology for an Experience.

Runtime Graph Registry

Provides the centralized institutional collection of Runtime Graph definitions.

Responsibilities include:

graph registration
graph discovery
graph lookup
centralized governance

The registry serves as the authoritative repository for Experience Runtime Graphs.

5. Relationship with the Integration Platform

The Integration Platform defines architectural relationships.

The Runtime Graph transforms those relationships into an execution topology.

Relationship:

Integration
        │
        ▼
Runtime Graph

Integration answers:

What connects?

The Runtime Graph answers:

How is execution organized?

6. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Platform defines execution operations.

The Runtime Graph defines execution structure.

Relationship:

Experience Runtime
        │
        ▼
Runtime Graph

The Runtime contract describes available operations.

The Runtime Graph describes the topology upon which those operations execute.

7. Relationship with the Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph becomes the primary input to the Runtime Orchestrator.

Relationship:

Runtime Graph
        │
        ▼
Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph owns no execution logic.

It provides only deterministic execution topology.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-07 → STEP-10
Experience Runtime Orchestrator

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-10 establishes the Experience Runtime Orchestrator, the institutional subsystem responsible for coordinating the complete operational execution of every COREI Experience.

While the Runtime Platform defines what operations are available, the Runtime Orchestrator defines how those operations are coordinated into a deterministic execution flow.

The Runtime Orchestrator does not implement business logic. It serves as the authoritative coordination layer for the Experience Platform.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime

3. Purpose

"How are all Experience subsystems coordinated into one deterministic operational execution?"

It is not:

Runtime implementation
Workflow Engine
Event Processing Engine
Dependency Injection Container
Scheduler
Business Logic Layer

Instead, it coordinates the Runtime contract already established within the Experience Platform.

4. Runtime Orchestrator Components

The Runtime Orchestrator introduces a single institutional coordination component.

Experience Runtime Orchestrator

Provides deterministic coordination of the Experience Runtime.

The Orchestrator delegates execution to the Runtime contract while preserving strict separation between coordination and implementation.

Supported operations include:

initialize()
load()
activate()
suspend()
resume()
unload()
shutdown()

Each operation delegates to the corresponding Runtime method.

The Runtime Orchestrator coordinates those operations.

Relationship:

Experience Runtime
        │
        ▼
Experience Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime owns execution contracts.

The Orchestrator owns execution coordination.

The Runtime Graph defines execution topology.

The Runtime Orchestrator consumes that topology during execution.

Relationship:

Runtime Graph
        │
        ▼
Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph contains no execution logic.

The Orchestrator contains no dependency definitions.

Both remain independent institutional subsystems.

Relationship:

Experience Runtime Orchestrator
        │

Workspace Platform

Relationship:

        │

Capability Platform

Provider Platform

STAGE-25 → PHASE-08 → STEP-01
Application Foundation

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-01 establishes the Application Foundation Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the core identity, governance, and runtime contract of every Application within the COREI Operating System.

While the Experience Platform represents an operational environment (Trading, Infrastructure, Risk, Research, Executive, Compliance), the Application Platform represents the operational modules that execute inside those Experiences.

The Application Foundation provides the canonical contracts, models, registry, manager, runtime contract, and public API required for all future Applications.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Experience Platform
        │
        ▼
Application Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Workspace Integration
        ├── Capability Integration
        ├── Provider Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Foundation layer establishes the institutional identity of every Application before introducing lifecycle, runtime, or integrations.

3. Purpose

The Application Foundation answers the institutional question:

"What is an Application within the COREI Operating System?"

It establishes:

Application identity
Application metadata
Application governance
Application registration
Application discovery
Runtime contract

It does not implement:

Business logic
UI rendering
Trading functionality
Infrastructure functionality
Runtime orchestration
State management

These responsibilities are introduced in subsequent steps.

4. Application Foundation Components

The Application Foundation introduces five institutional governance components.

Application Contract

Defines the canonical identity of an Application.

Each Application declares:

unique identifier
name
description

The contract becomes the authoritative definition for every operational module.

Application Model

Extends the Application Contract into the institutional Application model used throughout the platform.

The model becomes the common representation consumed by registries, managers, runtimes, and orchestration layers.

Application Registry

Provides the centralized collection of all registered Applications.

Responsibilities include:

application registration
application discovery
centralized governance
repository of operational modules

The Registry becomes the institutional source of truth for Application definitions.

Application Manager

Provides controlled access to the Application Registry.

Responsibilities include:

register()
getAll()
getById()

The Manager owns Application governance while the Registry remains a passive data repository.

Application Runtime Contract

Defines the standard runtime interface implemented by every Application.

Supported operations include:

initialize()
load()
activate()
suspend()
resume()
unload()
shutdown()

Application Lifecycle

This interface becomes the authoritative lifecycle definition for every operational module.

default operational behavior
platform standardization

The default implementation provides placeholder behavior until Application-specific runtime logic is introduced.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-08 → STEP-03
Application State

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-03 establishes the Application State Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the authoritative operational state model for every Application within the COREI Operating System.

While the Application Lifecycle Platform governs how an Application progresses through its operational lifecycle, the Application State Platform defines what operational condition an Application exposes at any point in time.

The State Platform provides a deterministic operational view while remaining independent of runtime execution and orchestration.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Application Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Workspace Integration
        ├── Capability Integration
        ├── Provider Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The State Platform becomes the third institutional layer of the Application Platform.

3. Purpose

The Application State Platform answers the institutional question:

"What is the current operational condition of an Application?"

It establishes standardized operational visibility while remaining independent of implementation.

The State Platform is not responsible for:

Runtime execution
Lifecycle transitions
Business logic
UI rendering
Provider initialization
Capability execution

Those responsibilities remain delegated to their respective institutional platforms.

4. Application State Components

The State Platform introduces five institutional governance components.

Application Availability

Defines whether an Application is operationally accessible.

Supported values include:

AVAILABLE
UNAVAILABLE

Availability represents operational accessibility rather than runtime health.

Application Health

Represents the overall operational health of an Application.

Supported values include:

HEALTHY
DEGRADED
UNHEALTHY

Health provides a standardized operational assessment independent of lifecycle state.

Application Readiness

Indicates whether an Application is prepared to perform its operational responsibilities.

Supported values include:

READY
NOT_READY

Readiness represents operational preparedness rather than execution status.

Application Status

Represents the current operational stage of an Application.

Supported values include:

INITIALIZED
LOADED
ACTIVE
SUSPENDED
UNLOADED
SHUTDOWN

Status reflects the current operational stage without dictating lifecycle transitions.

Application State

Aggregates the complete operational state into a single institutional model.

Each Application State contains:

Status
Health
Readiness
Availability

This becomes the authoritative operational representation consumed by higher-level runtime and orchestration systems.

5. Relationship with the Lifecycle Platform

The Lifecycle Platform governs operational progression.

The State Platform represents operational condition.

Relationship:

Application Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Application State

Lifecycle answers:

How does the Application progress?

State answers:

What is the Application's current condition?

Both remain independent institutional responsibilities.

6. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime modifies operational behavior.

The State Platform reports operational condition.

Relationship:

Application Runtime
        │
        ▼
Application State

Runtime execution updates state.

The State Platform itself performs no execution.

7. Relationship with the Experience Platform

Each Experience coordinates multiple Applications, each exposing an independent operational state.

Relationship:

Experience
        │
        ▼
Application State

This allows Experience-level monitoring without coupling state management across Applications.

Application Composition

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-04 establishes the Application Composition Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the structural organization of every Application within the COREI Operating System.

While the Application State Platform defines the operational condition of an Application, the Composition Platform defines how the Application is structurally assembled.

The Composition Platform introduces deterministic composition contracts without implementing rendering, runtime execution, or business logic.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Application Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime

        ├── Runtime Graph

The Composition Platform becomes the fourth institutional layer of the Application Platform.

3. Purpose

The Application Composition Platform answers the institutional question:

"How is an Application structurally organized?"

It defines:

application layout structure
logical regions
operational areas
component bindings

The Composition Platform is not responsible for:

UI rendering
runtime execution
lifecycle management
business logic
provider execution
capability execution

Those responsibilities remain delegated to later institutional platforms.

4. Application Composition Components

The Composition Platform introduces four institutional structural contracts.

Application Area

Defines the logical operational areas within an Application.

Supported areas include:

HEADER
CONTENT
SIDEBAR
FOOTER
OVERLAY

Areas provide standardized spatial classification independent of UI implementation.

Application Region

Represents a named structural region inside an Application.

Each region defines:

unique identifier
assigned Application Area

Regions become the reusable structural containers used by Applications.

Application Component Binding

Defines the relationship between Application components and structural regions.

Each binding specifies:

component identifier
destination region identifier

Bindings remain declarative and independent of rendering technology.

Application Composition

Represents the complete structural definition of an Application.

Each composition contains:

collection of regions
collection of component bindings

The Composition contract becomes the authoritative structural blueprint for every Application.

5. Relationship with the State Platform

The State Platform describes operational condition.

The Composition Platform describes structural organization.

Relationship:

Application State
        │
        ▼
Application Composition

State answers:

What is the Application's operational condition?

Composition answers:

How is the Application organized?

These remain independent institutional responsibilities.

Relationship:

Application Composition
        │
        ▼
Application Runtime

Composition owns structure.

Runtime owns execution.

Relationship:

        │
        ▼
Application Composition

The Composition Platform defines only the internal structure of an individual Application.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-08 → STEP-05
Application Runtime

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-05 establishes the Application Runtime Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the execution contract of every Application within the COREI Operating System.

While the Application Composition Platform defines how an Application is structurally organized, the Runtime Platform defines how an Application executes operationally.

The Runtime Platform introduces standardized execution contracts while remaining independent of orchestration, dependency management, integrations, and business logic.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Application Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Workspace Integration
        ├── Capability Integration
        ├── Provider Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Platform becomes the fifth institutional layer of the Application Platform.

3. Purpose

The Application Runtime Platform answers the institutional question:

"How does an Application execute?"

It establishes:

runtime execution contract
standardized operational methods
execution abstraction
runtime interface

The Runtime Platform is not responsible for:

lifecycle governance
runtime orchestration
dependency resolution
workspace integration
capability integration
provider integration
business logic
UI rendering

These responsibilities remain delegated to later institutional platforms.

4. Application Runtime Components

The Runtime Platform introduces two institutional execution components.

Application Runtime

Defines the canonical runtime contract implemented by every Application.

Supported operations include:

initialize()
load()
activate()
suspend()
resume()
unload()
shutdown()

This interface becomes the authoritative runtime definition for every operational Application.

Default Application Runtime

Provides the default implementation of the runtime contract.

Responsibilities include:

runtime conformance
execution standardization
placeholder implementation

The default runtime contains no business logic and serves as the institutional baseline until Application-specific runtime behavior is introduced.

5. Relationship with the Composition Platform

The Composition Platform defines structure.

The Runtime Platform executes that structure.

Relationship:

Application Composition
        │
        ▼
Application Runtime

Composition answers:

How is the Application organized?

Runtime answers:

How does the Application execute?

The two responsibilities remain completely independent.

6. Relationship with the Lifecycle Platform

Lifecycle defines operational progression.

Runtime performs operational execution.

Relationship:

Application Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Application Runtime

Lifecycle determines when operations occur.

Runtime determines what execution interface is available.

7. Relationship with the State Platform

Runtime execution produces operational state.

Relationship:

Application Runtime
        │
        ▼
Application State

The Runtime Platform performs execution.

The State Platform represents execution results.

Neither subsystem owns the other's responsibilities.

8. Repository Organization

The Runtime subsystem has been organized under:

src/applications/runtime/

Current repository structure:

application-runtime.ts

index.ts

Each file follows the COREI institutional principle of single responsibility.

9. Architectural Separation

The Runtime Platform owns:

execution contract
runtime interface
execution abstraction
default runtime implementation

It does not own:

runtime orchestration
dependency graph
workspace integration
capability integration
provider integration
lifecycle governance
business logic
rendering

These responsibilities remain delegated to later institutional layers.

10. Dependency Rules

The Runtime Platform follows the approved COREI dependency hierarchy.

Allowed:

Application Runtime
        ▲
        │
Application Lifecycle

Allowed:

Application Runtime
        ▲
        │
Application Composition

Allowed:

Application Runtime
        ▼
Application State

Forbidden:

Application Runtime
        │
        ▼
Workspace Platform

Forbidden:

Application Runtime
        │
        ▼
Capability Platform

Forbidden:

Application Runtime
        │
        ▼
Provider Platform

Those integrations will be introduced explicitly in later implementation steps.

11. Architectural Benefits

The Runtime Platform provides:

deterministic execution contracts
reusable runtime interface
implementation-independent execution model
standardized operational methods
institutional consistency
scalable execution architecture

Every future Application now executes through the same runtime contract.

Chart Application

initialize()
      │

The Runtime Platform standardizes execution contracts without prescribing implementation.

Every Application exposes a deterministic execution interface.
Runtime contracts remain independent of lifecycle governance, orchestration, integrations, and business logic.
Future runtime engines, schedulers, telemetry systems, replay engines, monitoring frameworks, and distributed execution environments can operate against a standardized runtime contract without modifying foundational interfaces.
The Application Platform preserves strict separation between identity, lifecycle, state, composition, runtime, integration, dependency modeling, and orchestration.

This maintains complete architectural symmetry with the Provider, Capability, Workspace, and Experience platforms.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-08 → STEP-06
Application Workspace Integration

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-06 establishes the Application Workspace Integration Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining how Applications integrate with the Workspace Platform within the COREI Operating System.

While the Application Runtime Platform defines how an Application executes, the Workspace Integration Platform defines how an Application participates within one or more Workspaces.

The Workspace Integration Platform introduces standardized integration contracts while remaining independent of Workspace implementation, rendering, runtime orchestration, and business logic.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Application Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Workspace Integration
        ├── Capability Integration
        ├── Provider Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Workspace Integration Platform becomes the sixth institutional layer of the Application Platform.

3. Purpose

The Application Workspace Integration Platform answers the institutional question:

"How does an Application connect to the Workspace Platform?"

It establishes:

Application-to-Workspace relationships
Workspace integration contracts
Integration registry
Public integration API

The Workspace Integration Platform is not responsible for:

Workspace implementation
Workspace runtime
Workspace layout
Workspace rendering
Application execution
Business logic

These responsibilities remain owned by the Workspace Platform.

4. Application Workspace Integration Components

The Workspace Integration Platform introduces two institutional integration components.

Application Workspace Integration

Defines the canonical relationship between an Application and a Workspace.

Each integration specifies:

Application Identifier
Workspace Identifier

The contract declares where an Application belongs, not how the Workspace behaves.

Application Workspace Integrations Registry

Provides the authoritative registry of all Application-to-Workspace relationships.

Responsibilities include:

storing integration definitions
centralized integration discovery
deterministic relationship management
reusable workspace associations

The registry contains only declarative mappings.

5. Relationship with the Workspace Platform

Applications execute inside Workspaces through the integration layer.

Relationship:

Application
        │
        ▼
Workspace Integration
        │
        ▼
Workspace Platform

The Application Platform declares the association.

The Workspace Platform owns the implementation.

6. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Platform executes an Application.

The Workspace Integration Platform determines which Workspace hosts that execution.

Relationship:

Application Runtime
        │
        ▼
Workspace Integration

Runtime execution remains independent of Workspace ownership.

7. Relationship with the Composition Platform

Composition defines internal Application structure.

Workspace Integration defines external Workspace placement.

Relationship:

Application Composition
        │
        ▼
Workspace Integration

Composition answers:

How is the Application organized internally?

Workspace Integration answers:

Where does the Application execute?

STAGE-25 → PHASE-08 → STEP-09
Application Runtime Graph

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-09 establishes the Application Runtime Graph Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for modeling the deterministic execution topology of every Application within the COREI Operating System.

While the previous integration layers define what an Application depends upon, the Runtime Graph defines how those dependencies are represented as a deterministic dependency graph.

The Runtime Graph is descriptive only. It does not execute Applications or initialize dependencies.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Application Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Workspace Integration
        ├── Capability Integration
        ├── Provider Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph becomes the ninth institutional layer of the Application Platform.

3. Purpose

The Application Runtime Graph answers the institutional question:

"What is the deterministic dependency topology of this Application?"

It establishes:

execution topology
dependency graph
graph nodes
graph edges
graph registry

The Runtime Graph is not responsible for:

runtime execution
dependency resolution
provider initialization
capability activation
workspace creation
lifecycle management
business logic

Those responsibilities belong to the Runtime Orchestrator and the respective institutional platforms.

4. Runtime Graph Components

The Runtime Graph Platform introduces four institutional components.

Application Runtime Node

Represents a single executable dependency within the Application graph.

Each node defines:

unique identifier
node type

Examples include:

Application
Workspace
Capability
Provider

Nodes provide the building blocks of the runtime topology.

Application Runtime Edge

Represents a deterministic relationship between two Runtime Nodes.

Each edge defines:

source node
target node

Edges describe dependency flow without executing any operations.

Application Runtime Graph

Represents the complete dependency topology for a single Application.

Each graph contains:

collection of Runtime Nodes
collection of Runtime Edges

The graph becomes the authoritative execution topology model.

Application Runtime Graph Registry

Provides the centralized registry for Runtime Graph definitions.

Responsibilities include:

graph registration
graph discovery
graph storage
deterministic lookup

The registry contains graph definitions only.

5. Relationship with the Integration Platforms

The Runtime Graph consolidates all previously declared integrations.

Relationship:

Workspace Integration
           │
           ▼
Capability Integration
           │
           ▼
Provider Integration
           │
           ▼
Application Runtime Graph

The Runtime Graph combines these declarative relationships into a single dependency topology.

6. Relationship with the Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Graph provides the execution topology.

The Runtime Orchestrator performs execution.

Relationship:

Application Runtime Graph
            │
            ▼
Runtime Orchestrator

The graph defines:

What depends on what

The Orchestrator determines:

When and how execution occurs

7. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Runtime Platform defines execution contracts.

The Runtime Graph defines execution topology.

Relationship:

Application Runtime
        │
        ▼
Application Runtime Graph

Runtime answers:

How does an Application execute?

Runtime Graph answers:

What execution dependencies exist?

Service Foundation

Unlike domain services such as Market Data Service, Order Service, Portfolio Service, or Risk Service, the Service Foundation does not implement business functionality. It defines the canonical infrastructure that every future service must inherit.

The Service Foundation provides:

service identity
service contract
service model
service registry
service manager
service runtime foundation
public platform API

This ensures that every service across the COREI ecosystem follows a single deterministic architectural standard.

        │
        ├── Provider Platform
        ├── Capability Platform
        ├── Workspace Platform

        ├── Application Platform
        └── Service Platform
                 │
                 ├── Foundation

                 ├── Runtime
                 ├── Provider Integration
                 ├── Capability Integration

The Service Foundation becomes the first institutional layer of the Service Platform.

3. Purpose

The Service Foundation answers the institutional question:

"What is a Service?"

It establishes:

Service identity
Service metadata
Service registration
Service discovery
Service management
Runtime contract
Public platform interface

The Service Foundation is not responsible for:

business logic
market operations
execution logic
data processing
service orchestration
runtime sequencing

Those responsibilities are introduced in later implementation steps.

4. Service Foundation Components

The Service Foundation introduces six institutional components.

Service Contract

The Service Contract defines the canonical identity of every Service.

Each Service provides:

unique identifier
service name
version

This contract becomes the minimum interface required for every Service in the COREI ecosystem.

Service Model

The Service Model extends the Service Contract into the canonical Service definition used internally by the platform.

The model provides a standardized representation for registration, management, runtime, and future orchestration.

Service Registry

The Service Registry provides the authoritative collection of all registered Services.

Responsibilities include:

centralized registration
service discovery
immutable lookup
deterministic registration order

The registry stores Service definitions only.

Service Manager

The Service Manager provides the management interface for interacting with the Service Registry.

Responsibilities include:

registering Services
retrieving registered Services
providing a controlled management boundary

The Service Manager becomes the institutional entry point for Service registration.

Service Runtime

The Service Runtime establishes the minimal runtime contract required by every Service.

At this stage the runtime defines only:

initialization
shutdown

Additional lifecycle operations will be introduced during later implementation phases.

Public Platform API

The Service Platform exports all foundational components through a single public interface.

This allows every future consumer to access the Service Platform without knowledge of its internal folder organization.

5. Repository Organization

The Service Platform has been organized under:

src/services/

Current repository structure:

src/services/

contracts/
models/
registry/
manager/
runtime/

index.ts

The Service Platform follows the same institutional organization as every previously implemented platform.

Every directory has a single institutional responsibility.

The Service Foundation owns:

Service identity
Service contract
Service model
Service registration
Service management
Runtime foundation
Platform exports

It does not own:

lifecycle
operational state
composition
runtime orchestration
provider integration
capability integration
application integration
business logic

Those responsibilities remain isolated for subsequent implementation steps.

STAGE-25 → PHASE-09 → STEP-02
Service Lifecycle

Status: COMPLETED

1. Objective

STEP-02 establishes the Service Lifecycle Platform, the institutional subsystem responsible for defining the canonical operational lifecycle of every Service within the COREI Operating System.

While the Service Foundation defines what a Service is, the Service Lifecycle defines how a Service progresses through its operational existence.

The Service Lifecycle introduces standardized lifecycle contracts while remaining independent of runtime execution, orchestration, dependency management, and business logic.

2. Architectural Position
COREI Operating System
        │
        ▼
Service Platform
        │
        ├── Foundation
        ├── Lifecycle
        ├── State
        ├── Composition
        ├── Runtime
        ├── Provider Integration
        ├── Capability Integration
        ├── Application Integration
        ├── Runtime Graph
        └── Runtime Orchestrator

The Lifecycle Platform becomes the second institutional layer of the Service Platform.

3. Purpose

The Service Lifecycle Platform answers the institutional question:

"How does a Service progress through its operational lifecycle?"

It establishes:

lifecycle contract
default lifecycle implementation
lifecycle public API

The Service Lifecycle Platform is not responsible for:

runtime execution
dependency initialization
provider interaction
capability interaction
application interaction
orchestration
business logic

Those responsibilities remain delegated to later institutional layers.

4. Service Lifecycle Components

The Lifecycle Platform introduces two institutional components.

Service Lifecycle

Defines the canonical lifecycle contract that every Service follows.

The lifecycle consists of four deterministic stages:

initialize()
start()
stop()
shutdown()

These operations establish the minimum operational lifecycle required by every COREI Service.

Default Service Lifecycle

Provides the institutional default implementation of the lifecycle contract.

At this stage the implementation contains no business behavior.

It exists to provide a standardized lifecycle implementation that future Services can extend or customize.

5. Lifecycle Flow

Every Service follows the same deterministic operational sequence.

initialize()
        │
        ▼
start()
        │
        ▼
stop()
        │
        ▼
shutdown()

Each transition is deterministic.

No stage may be skipped.

6. Relationship with the Runtime Platform

The Lifecycle defines what stages exist.

The Runtime performs how those stages execute.

Relationship:

Service Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Service Runtime

Lifecycle defines progression.

Runtime performs execution.

7. Relationship with the Runtime Orchestrator

The Runtime Orchestrator will later invoke lifecycle operations.

Relationship:

Runtime Orchestrator
        │
        ▼
Service Lifecycle

The Lifecycle exposes operations.

The Orchestrator determines execution order.

The Lifecycle subsystem has been organized under:

src/services/lifecycle/

Current repository structure:

default-service-lifecycle.ts

index.ts

service-lifecycle.ts

Each file follows the COREI institutional principle of single responsibility.

9. Architectural Separation

The Lifecycle Platform owns:

lifecycle contract
lifecycle definition
lifecycle implementation
lifecycle exports

It does not own:

runtime implementation
runtime coordination
provider initialization
capability execution
application execution
business logic

These responsibilities remain isolated within their respective platforms.

The Lifecycle Platform follows the approved COREI dependency hierarchy.

        │
        ▼
Service Runtime

Runtime Orchestrator
        │
        ▼
Service Lifecycle

Forbidden:

Service Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Business Logic

Forbidden:

Service Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Provider Platform

Forbidden:

Service Lifecycle
        │
        ▼
Capability Platform

The Lifecycle remains purely declarative.

11. Architectural Benefits

The Lifecycle Platform provides:

deterministic operational progression
reusable lifecycle contract
implementation-independent lifecycle definition
standardized lifecycle model
institutional consistency

Every future Service now follows the same lifecycle model.
